\\\"Conclusion: &lt;FOL formula>\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Use standard FOL notation with predicate-argument syntax: \\\"\\n    \\\"all x (P(x) -> Q(x)), exists x P(x), P(a) & Q(a), -P(a), P(a) | Q(a), But it HAS a chat_template in tokenizer_config.json, which means it supports the LLaMA 3.x chat format with `<|start_header_id|>`, `<|end_header_id|>`, `<|eot_id|>`. This is actually the Instruct-style tokenizer even though the directory says "3B" not "3B-Instruct". 

```text
The base model ships with the chat template tokenizer by default from Meta, so the SFT script's chat-formatted messages will work fine. Using the base model for SFT warmstart is actually preferable since we're teaching it new behaviors from scratch.

The training data has FOL premises and conclusions with Unicode logical symbols, and there's plenty of disk space available. The LLaMA tokenizer handles Unicode characters like ∀, ∃, →, ∧, ¬ without issues, so no special preprocessing is needed. Now I'm checking what existing SFT experiments look like in the registry to inform our approach.

Since I only have one GPU available, I'll use it for training with the existing SFT script and Llama-3.2-3B. My plan is to run a quick tokenizer test first, do a dry-run with just 3 steps to verify everything works, then submit the full training job.
```

```json
{
```
```json
  "server": "westd-39279",
  "command": "bash -c \"export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0; source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && python3 -c \\\"\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer\ntok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained('/root/autodl-tmp/models/Llama-3.2-3B')\ntests = [\n    'all x.(Dog(x) -> Animal(x))',\n    '∀x (EuropeanCountry(x) → Developed(x))',\n    '∃x (LiveIn(x, belgium) → Speak(x, french))',\n    'P(a) ∧ Q(a) ∧ ¬R(a)',\n]\nfor t in tests:\n    enc = tok.encode(t)\n    dec = tok.decode(enc, skip_special_tokens=True)\n    ok = t in dec or t.strip() == dec.strip()\n    print(f'OK={ok} | {t}')\n    if not ok:\n        print(f'  decoded: {dec}')\n\n# Test chat template\nmsgs = [\n    {'role': 'system', 'content': 'You are a FOL expert.'},\n    {'role': 'user', 'content': 'Formalize: All dogs are animals.'},\n    {'role': 'assistant', 'content': 'Premises:\\n1. ∀x (Dog(x) → Animal(x))\\nConclusion: ∀x (Dog(x) → Animal(x))'},\n]\nformatted = tok.apply_chat_template(msgs, tokenize=False)\nprint(f'\\nChat template works: {len(formatted) > 0}')\nprint(f'Template length: {len(formatted)} chars')\nprint(f'Pad token: {tok.pad_token}')\nprint(f'EOS token: {tok.eos_token}')\n\\\"\"\n",
  "account": "root"
}
```

````text
```
CUDA out of memory. Tried to allocate 48.00 MiB. 

GPU 0 has a total capacity of 94.97 GiB of which 24.88 MiB is free. Process 782092 has 53.52 GiB memory in use. Process 782644 has 31.09 GiB memory in use. Including non-PyTorch memory, this process has 10.32 GiB memory in use.
```

So the three processes were consuming 53.52 + 31.09 + 10.32 = 94.93 GiB, which fills the entire GPU. Those two processes must have been from the crash or other jobs that have since cleaned up.

Our actual process was PID 783232, not those other two PIDs. The OOM error happened when multiple processes were competing for GPU 0 — those two processes (782092 and 782644) were likely spawned from the same nohup session or from other experiments, and they've since terminated. The device mapping with `device_map={"": 0}` and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0` correctly routes everything to the single GPU, so that's not the issue.

Now that the GPU is clear with 97GB available, I should retry the dry-run. To be safer, I'll reduce the batch size — using `per_device_batch_size=2` with `gradient_accumulation_steps=4` keeps the effective batch size the same but spreads memory usage more conservatively. With 97GB VRAM and a 3B model taking roughly 6GB, there's plenty of headroom.
````

```text
The important thing is:
1. Training ran successfully for 3 steps (no OOM, no data issues)
2. The save error is because it's writing to /tmp and there's likely a filesystem/disk space issue there

For the actual training, we'll use `/root/autodl-tmp/outputs/d158_llama_sft_s42/` which is on the data disk with 79GB free.
```
